Castling is written in UCI as the king moving onto its own rook's square (e.g., 'e1h1' or 'b1a1'), exactly as it appears in 'get_legal_moves'.

This game is Chess960 (Fischer Random Chess): the pieces on the back rank start in a shuffled position, all other rules are the same as standard chess. After castling, the king and rook end up on the same squares as in standard chess (king on g-file and rook on f-file for kingside, king on c-file and rook on d-file for queenside). Castling is written in UCI as the king moving onto its own rook's square (e.g., 'e1h1' or 'b1a1'), exactly as it appears in 'get_legal_moves'.
Now is your turn to make a move. Before making a move you can pick one of the following actions:

You are a professional chess player and you play as black. This game is Chess960 (Fischer Random Chess): the pieces on the back rank start in a shuffled position, all other rules are the same as standard chess. After castling, the king and rook end up on the same squares as in standard chess (king on g-file and rook on f-file for kingside, king on c-file and rook on d-file for queenside). Castling is written in UCI as the king moving onto its own rook's square (e.g., 'e1h1' or 'b1a1'), exactly as it appears in 'get_legal_moves'.

After castling, the king and rook end up on the same squares as in standard chess (king on g-file and rook on f-file for kingside, king on c-file and rook on d-file for queenside). Castling is written in UCI as the king moving onto its own rook's square (e.g., 'e1h1' or 'b1a1'), exactly as it appears in 'get_legal_moves'.
